## 2026-11-08: Progress of SDK71 verification and private combination candidates

**v0.3.0 The overall goal is still ongoing.** The main working tree version is 0.2.2, the SDK overlay is 71, and 4,940 main tree inputs remain frozen. No versions have been submitted, pushed, upgraded, or released yet. The following private combination products are recorded separately from the original full results of the main tree.

- The original SDK71 Windows Debug execution covered 757 items: 743 passed, 4 failed, 10 skipped. The Windows ASan execution covered 758 items: 743 passed, 5 failed, 10 skipped. Four items failed together, resulting in Promise rejection, MessagePort, and two Headers WPT failures. ASan also experienced a 500 milliseconds timeout with Hono. The execution sequence for Release and Linux/LTO continued, followed by the automatic execution of a frozen set of 53 file combinations for the four configuration candidates. The current partial successes do not replace the complete results of these two rounds.
- The four independent clean ALL builds of the private 53 file combination have passed. After adding the 54 files input with Windows GenerationPool native observations, the actual instrumentation-dependent Windows ASan, Linux ASan/UBSan new builds have also been completed. The Windows directed regression has 21 passes with zero skips, including real DNS, TLS, process recovery, and queue stress tests. The Linux directed regression has 21 passes, 1 failure, and zero skips. They have not been merged into the main working tree, nor have they completed the final full configuration acceptance.
- The ordinary native fetch of raw/gzip, 13 class views, 5 reader construction paths, 2 reading APIs, in Windows Debug, Windows ASan, Linux ASan/UBSan, completed in groups 780, with 32,760 nested scenarios, matched the fixed default workerd test results for the same program by value. Independent review and verification of 65,520 real TCP responses, input separation, retries, EOF, source recycling before stop, and natural exit, as well as rejection of 4,680 file counterexamples. Additionally, the last byte of the real native upstream was changed from 16 to 17. Differences were observed in all three configurations of 132 readings, and they were rejected by the original model. This is sub-item evidence for R3; concurrency, context, abort, intrinsic, and the overall resource diagram are still under review.
- The same worker in Windows native ASan completed 1,800.16 seconds and 183,950 rounds of isolated channel lifecycle testing. The number of isolated channel callbacks was zero, and the worker naturally exited. This demonstrates that channel reuse does not equal a complete host/network/state soak or native LeakSanitizer.
- The failure to exit the Linux strict sandbox for TLS is still retained: the diagnosis of the non-modified worker exposes LeakSanitizer, which cannot initiate the tracer; the independent minimum programs only prohibit clone or ptrace, both of which fail to reproduce the issue, and the baseline leak check passes. Neither the security policy nor the sanitizer checks have been relaxed, and there is no claim of leakage or a claim that strict ASan has passed. Clang's function checker report for the type erase callback in OpenSSL 3.5.7 is also retained, and the pass for GCC and Windows does not dismiss this finding.
- The Hono observation program measures the initial route construction to be approximately 468 – 494 Milliseconds, close to the original 500 Millisecond budget. The timestamp-driven approach can still reproduce the original timeouts. The private build of QuickJS optimization levels is rejected by MSVC with /O1, which is incompatible with the existing /RTC1. There are no qualified optimization candidates yet. Original failures and all existing checks remain. The root cause has not been closed yet.
- R6 historical binary identity audit has been corrected: a previous misinterpretation of the reference report was rectified. The failed single-worker reports actually recorded both host and worker summaries. The original host with identical summaries has been found; 47 workers from 229 ELF workers and 242 package archives have been checked according to the correct worker summaries. No original worker has been restored, and only the findings in this area have been reported. The DATA discard branch flaw in the reconstruction of SDK59 archives has been causally reproduced, but it does not fully replicate the original binary pair's cause.

For details on identities, scope, and original proof summaries, please refer to [Progress of this round of integration](../../evidence/v0.3.0/2026-11-08/combined-native-progress.json) This document addresses the issue of storing data in private candidate files. After the main tree is frozen, the data will be merged back in and re-qualified. The completed tasks for R1/R2/R4 remain unchanged. R3/R5/R6/R7/R8 and R9 continue to be available. Supported native platforms, real Hosted CI environments, complete sanitizer/fuzz/state/soak, the official three-way negotiation process for the same quiet session, cold/QPS, real 2, GiB density, and 1280/960/820/600 with four viewports still need to be verified. The official performance targets for 0, T14–T17 have not been completely disabled.

### This round of mainline fixes and verification

`src/worker_runtime.cc`: Snapshots without Promise callbacks. `PendingWrite`: Skipped. `JS_Call`: Retains the token scope and release of the actual Promise callback. `update_poll`: Excludes shutdowns, unstarted, and closing polls; shutdown is cleared synchronously. `poll_started_`: Current runtime source SHA256 is. `e84a2e58510c027f2f8f6ddd4255d5253aa70729fc45663646d08d3f77b52ca6`.

Formal counterexamples are run prior to merging: 8 out of 21 CTests failed. Snapshot timeouts/cancellations result in TypeError and cause Workers to exit 1; **The healthy second request in the formal counterexamples is actually completed**, and the previous statements claiming it destroys the second request should be based on this complete observation. Issues with closing polls are reproduced using independent batches without HTTP or PendingWrite: Windows ordinary and ASan trigger handle closures when processing metrics after SHUTDOWN, Linux Release naturally exits in the same sequence; all control of metrics before shutdown is successful.

Added 7 formal tests: `worker_response_retirement_{timeout,cancel,grant,shutdown,contract_controls}` and `worker_poll_shutdown_{before,after}`. The teardown matrix maintains a duration of 750ms, a window size of 32 bytes, and a payload of 33/101 bytes, covering snapshots/real streaming Promises, complete protocols and quotas, healthy reuse, and natural exit. The private C++ candidate passed 165 adjacency CTests, and after merging, 21 new combinations passed smoke testing. Additionally, the original 480 combinations were refreshed, along with 21 new combinations.

The 19 failures that did not occur during the first mainline execution without Hono are retained: 3 failures in Linux cross-request tests without report directories, and a total of 16 public reference tests with two non-ASan configurations missing `CAPSID_WORKERD_REF`. The runner was corrected and the 19 tests were rerun item by item in a new directory, all passed. The original commands, application budgets, assertions, and failure logs are retained. Subsequently, 12 Hono runs were conducted quietly with three configurations in series, all passed. The original 500ms application budget is retained. The current qualification is calculated as 501 based on different combinations. A total of 562 mainline executions were carried out this round, with 535 passed and 27 failed (8 formal product counterexamples and 19 runner failures). The 21 smoke tests and 19 fixes reruns are counted as execution attempts. The 165 private candidate regression tests are recorded separately.

### Private JS branch: GC has a local proof, and open differences remain after cross-request fixes

The 19 input files and three sets of historical binaries from the old v7 remain unchanged. The current C++ code is used to construct the private v7 and v8-context; the main branch remains JS v5. Both v7 and v8 have 72 scenarios for GC resource policies. These scenarios are tested through real WeakRefs, FinalizationRegisters, and bounded allocation pressure, covering raw/gzip, tee/clone/nested, fully idle/pending/abort scenarios. Each round of independent replaying resulted in 2934 consistent rewriting counterexamples; v8 passed the recheck. The GC evidence is located in `build-v030-dev/evidence/fetch-branch-lifetime-20261107/progress-audit.json` and `v8-progress-audit.json`. The fixed Windows-based bounded GC probes remain accessible to both branches and open at the source after the sentinel has been reclaimed. This part is recorded according to the product resource strategy, and the public GC equivalence has not yet been proven. The combination of binding, more concurrency/context, unstarted branches, quota, and decoding errors still requires further investigation.

New real dual WebSocket context probes: Two sets of fixed worker configurations set the tee branch across requests to `ReadableStreamSource`. The `reader.closed` rejects and allows release. Version 7 returns a Canceller error in 36 single-read scenarios with pending records and rejects release. Private v8-context fixes these 36 targeted errors/closed/release behaviors; the independent model rejects 1674 counterexamples, and the 18 complete public states remain consistent. The 18 gzip cases still have differences in sequential reading segments: the reference end is 27+5 bytes, while the private implementation is 32 bytes, and this difference is retained.

Extending to release when no reading is performed, canceling reader/stream, continuous reading by the same reader, after values and pipeTo, there are still public state differences in three configurations of 54 products, with 51 still having open status differences. Two sets of actual reference observations, 36, are consistent with each other. This extension only completes the original collection, identity verification, and direct comparison; the independent action model still needs to be completed. All source connections are closed before stopping the Host, with healthy requests and natural exits being processed. See `build-v030-dev/evidence/fetch-branch-context-20261107/progress-audit.json` and `action-baseline-audit.json` for details. **v8 remains private, and the requirement for branch ownership remains open.**

### The following implementation sequence

1. The new private copy amendment branch crosses requests for release/cancel, pending reads, and iterator errors are retained. With two sets of actual reference, independent action models, and counterexample verification, the existing single read and GC scenarios are rerun.
2. Continuing with the coding of the gzip branch prototype. There are 30 differences in the old unread matrix, and another 18 segment differences in the current gzip context matrix. ### Main working tree merged: 4 C API test helpers returned with response quota

`tests/test_native_fetch_deadline.cc`, `tests/test_fetch_response_limits.cc`, `tests/test_worker_channel_discard.cc`, `tests/test_worker_v4_negotiation.cc` are now passed through `tests/response_credit_probe.h`, and `capsid_worker_grant_response_credit` is called for each actual consumption of `RESPONSE_BODY` bytes. This API only queues quota frames locally and does not actively flush them. The channel-discard retains the barrier order of observing CANCEL, then sending DATA and ACK temporarily. The original call parameters, deadlines, and protocol assertions are retained; the explicit `--small-response-window` mode is used to supplement cross-window observation.

| New command suffix (unified prefix helper_response_credit_) | Initial Response Window | Actual Coverage | CTest deadline |
| --- | ---: | --- | ---: |

| limits / limits_encoded | 256 bytes | Each retains 320 original quotas, shards, cancellations, encoded-header scenarios | Each session lasts for 60 seconds. |
| discard | 16 bytes | 12 cancellations and 12 reuse requests for health checks | 30 seconds |

Additionally, reviewed the complete semantic state of 1920 quota scenarios and 90 deadline scenarios, which remained consistent with the previous iteration.

The new verifier initially treated all response headers as fixed-body, resulting in 3 failed real CTest reviews. The source code rules are as follows: If the body does not exceed the initial credit, it should be set to fixed-body; otherwise, it must be set to streaming header. Flags= 0, credit= 0. Amend to adopt this definitive rule, add 24 metadata counterexamples, with controls rejecting 109 error records per configuration. All 24 revised observations have passed, and all 24 unlimitated old observations remain rejected. The original failures with 3 errors of expectation were not covered as successes. For independent review, please refer to `upgrade58bry-model-replay.json`, `upgrade58bsj-full-response-review.json`.

This round of official CTest consisted of 75 attempts: 57 were successful, 15 failed due to no quota counterexamples, and 3 failed due to incorrect response header models. Only 42 successful commands were retained for qualification, and the remaining 15 were replaced by executions after the model was corrected. Over the course of SDK69, a total of 1526 attempts were made: 1466 were successful, 60 failed in history and were retained, and 986 successful attempts were replaced by subsequent runs; 1466−986=480. Private direct executions and model assertions are not counted as additional CTest combinations.

The default 256KiB quota consumption for the front-end `tests/test_native_fetch_response_limits.cc` is also retained. The actual response sizes of 262143/262144/262145/786469 bytes, with three configurations having 12 responses each, and 57 controls per configuration, are still included in the current selection according to their original evidence range, as specified in `upgrade58brh-main69-credit-progress-audit.json`. Four subsequent assistant tasks have been completed; however, it cannot be claimed that all C API consumers or runtime error paths have been perfected.

### New discovery: Snapshot text will be corrupted due to timeout/cancellation after partial transmission, leading to runtime exception states.

The native snapshot write of `PendingWrite.resolve/reject` is equivalent to `JS_UNDEFINED`, but `resolve_pending/reject_pending` still directly calls it. `TypeError: not a function` is generated in case of timeout or cancellation; the old 5 second timeout observation also resulted in an assertion of `UV_HANDLE_CLOSING` with Windows ASan. The source code shows that even after shutting down the poll, the subsequent output path may still call update_poll. The connection between the two paths must be isolated independently, and it cannot be claimed that the causal proof of a handle issue is complete just because the symptoms have disappeared.

The private C++ candidate is located in `upgrade58bsa-candidate/worker_runtime.cc`. Only snapshot callbacks that do not exist are skipped, and the process of restarting the closed poll is prevented. ## Round 102 (2026-10-21): Large-scale reading, DefaultReader allocation, and EOF offset

This round of corrections addresses the discrepancies in actual readings beyond the initial window, and corrects two instances of real-world reference errors. Locked successfully on Windows and Linux systems: **417/417**. Two issues were resolved: **401 cases were approved, and 16 cases failed**. Among these, **6 cases involved issues with DefaultReader backing capacity**, and **10 cases had non-zero input offsets in BYOB scenarios**. The original reference data, labeled `upgrade56e-*`, was reviewed carefully in detail using `upgrade56f-baseline-case-review.json`. The initial verifier mistakenly recorded EOF offsets as 16; however, actual references returned 0 in all cases. The previous results were not revised, but the corrected samples, labeled `upgrade56g-*`, now show 417 successful cases on both platforms.

The final product only requires modifications to `js/websocket.js`. The native byte source automatically allocates a DefaultReader buffer of **4096** bytes. It copies data to the controller's BYOB view and calls `respond()`; when the view cannot accommodate the entire transfer fragment, it retains the remaining portion and returns the credit based on the actual number of copied bytes. Only by configuring automatic allocation and then `enqueue()` will the original fragment continue to be exposed, and this fix cannot be completed. The native BYOB returns an empty EOF view that is the same as the transferred backing view, with a zero-length byte offset of **0**. The backing capacity and outer padding values are retained. Limiting the wrapping layer to transfer the existing correct backing results in no changes to `js/fetch.js`.

The official list consists of **417 = 330 items of native view + 56 items of standard JS comparison + 10 items of type borrowing + 21 items of native body**, while the original 396 items and the independent 46 items of transition are retained. The new 21 item includes a DefaultReader with five construction paths, the empty/short body 10 items, five paths of BYOB minimum/readAtLeast, 10 items, as well as a large-scale DefaultReader 1 item. All large-scale responses are **1 MiB + 37 bytes**. BYOB requires a complete response for a single input offset 16, separation of two inputs, an empty EOF, complete sequential bytes and SHA, outer bytes, the same VM identity, and a normal conclusion. The actual size crosses **64 KiB** of the initial window.

## Round 101 (2026-10-21): Reader prototype borrowing, fixes for Float16Array and ABI testing

This round has completed the two priority reviews from the previous round. Real-world tests on Windows and Linux have confirmed that the old product would contaminate the cancellation reason when using another type of reader's `cancel()` method, and would reject incorrect recipients. The `Float16Array` would incorrectly reject normal reads due to missing element width information. Fixes resulted in **376 passes, 20 failures / 396 items** across both platforms, which corresponds to **10 prototype borrows and 10 Float16Array cases**. Both platforms have locked the reference at **396/396**. The original failure exits, individual reasons, and binary identities are retained in `upgrade55e-*` and `upgrade55f-baseline-case-review.json`, and are not marked as passed.

The product only requires modifications to `js/websocket.js`: Record independent weak reference types. Before parameter reading, context checking, and modifying suspension states, cross-class prototype borrowing between Default/BYOB readers is prohibited. The type records remain valid after `releaseLock()`, and the active I/O context is still unassociated as usual. Errors in `read/readAtLeast/closed/releaseLock/cancel` synchronous operations throw a fixed-reference `Illegal invocation` exception, which does not trigger option getters, does not separate input, and does not change the subsequent normal cancellation reasons. The element width is derived from the `byteLength/length` getters of the TypedArray captured at startup, overriding `Float16Array`. It does not depend on constructors or width properties that can be changed by the application. This round does not claim that all error recipient boundaries, such as ordinary objects, fake instances, and Proxy, have been completed.

The official BYOB list has increased from **306 to 396 items**, retaining the old **250 native items, 56 standard JS comparison items**, and **46 independent transition items**. Eight new views of `Int8Array/Uint8ClampedArray/Int16Array/Uint32Array/Float16Array/Float32Array/BigInt64Array/BigUint64Array` have been added, covering default/min2/min3/readAtLeast2/readAtLeast3 and held/EOF. There are **80 items** in total, all using `getReader`. The five construction path matrices of the original five views are retained. Additionally, **10 new native reader type checks** have been added, covering two types of readers and five construction methods, and checking the borrowing behavior before and after release. Currently, there are **330 native views, 56 standard comparison items, and 10 prototype borrows**; the native view family consists of **13 types (including DataView)**, not all of which have been tested with five construction methods.

Literal contracts and independent control over rejecting error reports **7058/7058**; actual complete six-file gate control **477/477**, including a single file for overall gate control **223/223**. Added Float16 separation, cancellation reasons, synchronization exceptions, and counterexample checks after release, and it is required to have additional grouped reports for actual existence. The composite backend report is only used for verifier control; the product passes from the following actual CTests.

## Round 100 (2026-10-21): Upgrade rollback of native BYOB base matrix for HTTP response body

This round of modifications involves `js/websocket.js` and `js/fetch.js`. The Upgrade/Regression HTTP data channel now provides a byte source; native BYOB reading converts to a minimum size based on the element width of the original view, and returns a `Uint8Array` of the same range, preserving the input offset, backing buffer, and separation behavior. Non-empty EOF tail blocks that are less than the minimum size retain `done=false`; the native `closed` signal for normal completion is delayed until an empty EOF reading or cancellation. Explicit completion is performed when the byte source is closed; the ACK length is saved before byte enqueue, limiting the ability to retain bytes associated with native streams. Standard JS byte streams retain their view type, and the positive call path for `readAtLeast()` is added.

## Round 99 (2026-10-20): Direct DefaultReader construction path for native body

This round of fixes has addressed the confirmed gap in reader association in `js/websocket.js`. The original implementation only associated the native body with `body.getReader()` when reading was suspended; now, the same association is achieved directly, while maintaining the instance validation of the original constructor, `newTarget`, and prototype relationships. Global construction, `prototype.constructor`, subclasses, and `Reflect.construct` have all been subjected to actual verification. The standard JS stream continues to use its original queuing reading behavior.

The formal fetch transition has been increased from **30 items to 46 items**, with the original 30 items retained, and four new constructs added, each with three native behaviors and one standard flow control. The native items include checks for object cancellation, cancellation before the first read, and suspension of the second read. The standard flow control involves queuing two reads followed by cancellation, requiring both to complete normally. The new constructs also verify the identity of instances, prototypes, constructors, and prototype constructors. Original verifications such as byte, reason type, Promise response order, actual TCP termination, same VM, input, and binary identity continue to be effective.

## Round 98 (2026-10-20): Optimizing real-time allocation probes and complete spawn exception traversal in the build process

In this round, Root only modified `tests/test_abi_guard.cc`. After the original allocator replacement function was inlined under GCC `-O3 -Werror`, it was diagnosed as belonging to a different allocation family due to the implementation of malloc/free and array allocation delegation. As a result, four allocation entries and eight release entries were reserved for non-inlined probes. The warnings, optimizations, and normal allocation/release pairing remain unchanged. The intermediate candidates that only failed to compile at the release entry were retained. Private verifications under six actual SDKs were completed with nine modes, no parameters, and unknown mode rejections before Root was integrated.

The formal nine-mode now injects `bad_alloc` and common C++ exceptions into each allocated point that is successfully spawned. The common exceptions must actually occur, and according to the observed nothrow exception handling requirements, the exceptions must be precise in terms of `OUT_OF_MEMORY / INTERNAL_ERROR`, empty output pointers, non-empty errors, and resource conservation. The necessity of this is evident in real-world sensitivity counterexamples: private runtimes that only remove common exception cleanup during construction still pass through the original coverage, and extended coverage checks leak handles. This correction does not change any product source code, quotas, or protocols.

| Current Main Build | ALL / Package | This round of ABI, protocol, strategy, registration, and documentation review | There is evidence of gate access |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Windows Ordinary Debug | Pass | 23/23 | Follow the complete 97th round with 61/61 |
| Windows ASan Debug | Pass | 23/23 | Follow the complete 97th round with 61/61 |
| Linux UID 1000, build type is empty | Pass | 23/23 | Follow the complete 97th round of 77/77 |

In this round, the total count is **69/69**, with zero failures and zero skips. A comparison was made with the non-probe products archived in the previous round. Only the files related to the ABI allocation model changed. The SUT/auxiliary binaries, commands, and configurations used for the gate were unchanged, so **199 complete gate** test evidence was retained. Five product source code corrections and four test/registration corrections have been integrated: temporary strings/ containers used in strategy compilation can throw an empty constructor; snapshot reserved capacity and in-place construction of environment entries; internal result types using throwable copies, to avoid implicit `noexcept` moves in Debug STL from terminating upon allocation failure. ## Round 93 (2026-10-20): The FiveStack QPS controller has been connected to the archiver producer, with full strong compatibility ongoing.

This round replaces the old QPS shell with the new Capsid managed/strict, fixes the workerd, FastAPI, Flask, and PHP to form a complete three-workload control chain, and finalizes the formal archiving contract. **The full Linux/Windows, 0.3.0, and strong compatibility have not yet been completed. T15/T16/T17 has not been completely shut down, and no performance benchmarks can be released yet.** Currently, it has been defaulted to five rounds, with each round consisting of 5 seconds of preheating, followed by 15 seconds of measurement, and 64/64 and 30000 milliseconds of request deadlines. The old three-round/managed opt-in status is only retained in the historical records below.

The implementation entry is `bench/qps-lane.sh` → `qps-lane.py`, the adapter is `qps_probe.py`, the input archive is `qps_inputs.py`, and the shared files include `measurement_runtime.py` and `qps_control.py`. The same owner manages the service root and an independent session for each Go client; both PHP's nginx/FPM double roots contain all their descendants. PSS only calculates the fixed service forest, excluding controllers and Go. If one root fails to clean up, it continues to recycle other roots and retains the failed state; it does not recover subreaper in advance or consider omissions as successes.

Each stack first undergoes trickle processing with a fresh process, and then the process is fully reclaimed before starting another loaded process. At each stage, the availability of listeners, a fixed single worker topology, and a precise JSON-1k application readiness response are verified. This response is not counted towards the 150 trickles. Trickle processing is scheduled at a monotonous clock rate of 10 Hz, with state, complete body summaries, and timing being written out sequentially. Failure occurs if the delay exceeds 0.1 seconds. The loaded process starts a real Go tool for each workload/round, preserving the client PID/start tick, two-stage raw data, and verdict. The final settle process lasts for 1 second, with a maximum of eight iterations at one-second intervals. Two equal positive PSS values are required to pass. The protocol is not relaxed to pass the smoke test.

## Round 91 (2026-10-20): Release of compiler mount disk and fix for temporary file ownership. Maintain full compatibility.

This round of closure addresses the specific compiler defects listed in the previous round: In Linux, when the mount output directory does not support `RENAME_NOREPLACE`, the temporary name is removed using `linkat` in the same directory. Failed diagnostics retain the immediately obtained `errno` or Win32 error code. **The full Linux/Windows 0.3.0 strong compatibility has not yet been completed. T15/T16/T17 has not been completely closed.** The modifications made this round concern the release tools, testing, and their build dependencies. The 153 runtime/Host implementation inputs in `src/js/include` remain unchanged.

The backup path is only used for `EINVAL`, `ENOSYS`, `EOPNOTSUPP`/`ENOTSUP`, and does not retry and overwrite existing target, permissions, read-only, space, or general I/O errors. The native file system has successfully tested renaming; the WSL E drive `v9fs` still returns `EINVAL=22` in tests, but atomic creation of hard links is successful, and the existing target returns `EEXIST=17` with content preserved. The new compiler directly performs real regression through 8/8 groups on the mounted drive, as seen in `upgrade45h-mounted-publication.json`. Implementation basis: [rename(2)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/rename.2.html), [link(2)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/link.2.html).

The reviewer also found that the old implementation would clean up temporary files that were not created during this call after the exclusive creation of temporary files failed. Now, the creation ownership of the three temporary files and the three official files is recorded separately, with only the output of this call being cleaned up. When a hard link is published but deleting the temporary name fails, the ownership of the official file is still retained for rollback purposes. If the cleanup fails, a separate report is generated, and no claim is made that the rollback is complete. The three independent output paths do not have atomicity in case of power failure or process crash; deployment requires the outer generation staging directory.

Real tests do not use fake compilers to replace the product: new output and determinism, independent SHA-256 and signature message encoding, existing files/directories at each output location, subsequent directories that are missing or not directories, two real compilers competing, physical path aliases, pre-existing temporary files, and Linux formal output symbolic links all have assertions. Windows pre-existing temporary file cases are tested by creating a child process that pauses this test, prepares files with its real PID, then restores and verifies the content; Linux uses its own fork/exec child process. Only the processes and directories owned by this test are recycled.

## Round 89 (2026-10-20): Fixing cold C-ABI probes. The entire cold/QPS lane is still pending connection.

This round has continued the fix of the cold start C-ABI measurement tool from round 88; **The complete Linux/Windows 0.3.0 strong compatibility has not yet been completed, and T15/T16/T17 has not been completely shut down.** The final audit of the density controller has been completed as `upgrade42-final-audit.json`, and the observation of the small pool has not been changed to a 2 GiB capacity measurement.

The old tool actually produced red examples: HTTP 500 errors, errors/truncation/extra body, and failed preheating still resulted in successful lines; `--iterations 0/-1/1junk` was accepted; SIGINT/SIGTERM left unreclaimed workers. The original output is saved in `upgrade43d-tests.stderr.log` (7 groups, 12 failures, 2 errors), and the old source code and binary are also retained. The original tool's `-Wcomment` compilation failure is recorded separately as `upgrade43c-build-failed.json`, and compilation failures are not considered as red examples.

Fixing the code located in `bench/cold-start.cc` and the independently testable `bench/cold_start_measurement.h`:

- Using existing public APIs `capsid_worker_pid()` Locate the actual worker and record the PID/start tick. No longer infer the child process's identity from inheriting SO_PEERCRED from socketpair.
- For the first warm request and each subsequent one, the request ID is verified, along with an HTTP 200 response header. Every byte of the frozen content is also checked. If there are error states, exceptions, timeouts, reordering issues, or an incorrect number of bytes, the request immediately fails, and no successful lines are output during this iteration.
- `total_ms` is truncated after the first completed verification response. The process tree's findings, warm state, settlement, and PSS are not included in the cold start time calculation. The warm state is defaulted to 50 attempts, with the median of even samples taking the average of the two middle values. The number of delayed attempts and correct responses are both written to the original row.
- After the initial response, the entire worker hierarchy remains intact until it is moved to the warm state. Members that have been observed but then re-allocated are retained. Attempts to remove, recycle, replace, or add new members fail. The PSS can be set to eight levels at most, with a one-second interval between checks. It is necessary to obtain two equal positive readings, while maintaining the identity and readings of each process.
- SIGINT/SIGTERM, request failures, and timeouts are terminated through the same C-ABI RAII to recycle the worker. This is the measured abortive destroy; no claim is made to normal exit, complete cleanup of all descendants, or verification of native leaks. The entire lane still requires a unified external process tree controller.

The independent zero tick record `upgrade43i-stat-zero-red.json` also detected a new parser that misinterprets integer value 0 as a missing/overflow issue, which has been corrected and incorporated into the final C++ model group. The requirement that PSS must be positive has not been relaxed.

## Round 88 (2026-10-20): Density controller, actual coverage, and archive producer are connected

This round has rewritten the control layer for the density lanes, integrating **managed/strict, worker-based coverage, recursive PSS, controlled cleanup, and T16 archive producer**. **The completion of T15 as a whole, actual 2 GiB density/three lanes per session, and full Linux/Windows 0.3.0 compatibility is still pending.** During runtime, the 153 implementation inputs in `src/js/include` remain unchanged; no new product compilation or native sanitizer qualifications were added this round.

| This round of actual execution | The selected tool: CTest | New Python Testing Team added | Current Registration Count |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Windows Normal Debug Configuration | 5/5 | 21 passed, 10 Linux-specific groups skipped | 542 |
| Windows ASan Debug Configuration | 5/5 | Same as above, belonging to script testing | 543 |
| Linux UID 1000 Debug Configuration | 6/6 | 31/31, passed real child processes and generator tests | 768 |

The 15 sets of evidence verification for the 87th round were additionally run and verified; the number of new groups in the three modules is **13 + 13 + 5 = 31**. The 10 skipped items in Windows come from `/proc`, while Linux has sub-processes/formal producers as prerequisites. This is an internal platform division within the script, not an update that skipped all 23 items in the previous full matrix, nor can it be considered a native Windows PSS or an additional C++ ASan coverage. The actual testing entry is `upgrade42aa-{native,native-asan,linux}.json`, corresponding to JUnit/raw outputs.

The density shell interface now directly executes Python, no longer using a sub-shell to save port counts/PID lists. The controller retains the port lease and PID/start-time identities, ensuring that the listening sockets belong to the current process. It collects all descendants with active PSS, requiring two equal readings. After becoming READY, the process members are fixed, and subsequent exits, reclamation, or replacements result in measurement failures, preventing the sampling set from being reduced. The cleanup process first freezes the own process tree, terminates in the order of descendants to parent processes, and reclaims orphan processes. Sub-processes, listeners, and reused PIDs unrelated to the current process are protected. This is a forced reclamation after measurement, not evidence of normal product exit or leakage checks.

The search will fail if there is an overbudget situation. Zero/indecipherable PSS, incorrect content, incomplete coverage, or process death cannot be considered as capacity boundaries. Even if there is only one worker or the cap has been reached, a final verification must be performed again. Drift can be retried a maximum of two times. Stability issues that exceed the budget can be retried eight times at a time. Failure occurs if there is a timeout or exhaustion. The cap is clearly marked as a lower boundary. Each generated fixture identifies the worker locally through request records with a benchmark nonce. All response checks are fixed in bytes. Normal health checks are not counted towards coverage. No diagnostic fields are added to the public response or production IPC.

Actual verification is kept separate:

- The workerd was successfully connected to the current managed/strict, and it passed through 1/2, worker/cell, cold/warm, and in total 8 times. See `upgrade42f-probe-smoke.json` for more details.
- The public Bash/Python entry for the **final code** has completed 12 searches/final probes, with 4 10k small pool observation units. All 173 files have been recalculated and summarized, as shown in `upgrade42ab-cli-smoke.json`. These only verify the small pool control chain **and are not measurements of 2 GiB capacity or new performance release points**.
- Ultimately, the CLI failed to receive any data from SIGINT/SIGTERM. All controllers/host/worker were also disconnected. There was no valid data available for analysis. See `upgrade42ac-cli-cancel.json` for more details.
- The complete 12-unit formal documentation testing was conducted by injecting synthetic probes into the temporary repository. Actual source identity/binary checks, ports, searches, file writing, sealing, and real-time verifiers were performed. After the shared APPS were modified, the frozen inputs were maintained at their original values. Synthetic numbers were not included in the data.js file.

Three sets of counterexamples actually caught and corrected the controller's own issues: leaving behind child processes when the process is thrown or interrupted before the identity is registered after startup (`upgrade42j-*` red, `upgrade42k-*` green); creating a race condition in OUT that writes failed.json into another round's directory (`upgrade42l-*` red, `upgrade42m-*` green). The failure records and the source code before correction were saved, with no assertions being deleted or failures being classified as successes.

Final real-machine counterexample proof: When the root process is still alive but the child processes have exited and been reclaimed, the sampling will miss it (`upgrade42w-*` red); this scenario fails clearly after fixing the READY member, `upgrade42x-*` green. After correction, the complete tool CTest is actually run again as `upgrade42aa-*`; the initial configuration is reused for the first round `upgrade42r-*`; the final entry/abandonment is also rerun as `upgrade42ab-*` and `upgrade42ac-*` without re-naming the initial round results.

The formal density requires metadata from actual clean builds/quiet sessions; by default, it is set to managed/strict. Missing metadata results in a rejection before startup. `--observation` is explicitly non-publishable. The generated input is first verified and snapshotted using the freeze generator. The runner/generator source code and Python/taskset identities are saved with the archive, and verified again before termination. Generated configurations/logs are exclusive to this round and cannot overwrite shared APPS or existing OUTs. For complete usage/bounds, please refer to [Density Producer Protocol](../../../bench/perf-evidence.md#density-producer).

**The newly identified cold start defects require priority remediation.** In `bench/cold-start.cc`, the `worker_peer_pid()` function creates a socketpair for the parent process, which is then passed to the child process to read the SO_PEERCRED. The child process then uses this PID to perform PSS. In `src/client.cc`, the socket[0] on the parent side is explicitly exposed as a worker fd. The Linux documentation indicates that this credential comes from the moment the socketpair is created. Independent testing on a physical machine showed that the creation PID was 13, the actual child process PID was 14, and the credential returned by the system was still 13, as seen in `upgrade42u-cold-pid-diagnosis.json`. This confirms that the mechanism and source code path are correct. **No cold tooling was run/implemented in this round, nor did any impact on the old value calculation occurred.** The public `capsid_worker_pid()` exists in the ABI, and it should be used to determine the worker root, followed by verification of its subtrees. Refer to [Linux unix(7)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/unix.7.html).

The overall evidence entry is `build-v030-dev/evidence/native-asan-qualification-20261018/upgrade42-final-audit.json`. The Root is 4486, the input is HEAD still `8028b88adff26cbc1abcb9a2fafce2ffc3d8090f`, the version is 0.2.2, and the patch is 58. The result document is updated and recorded separately after the final test, without any Root submitting/pushing/releasing. Historical frozen source code, binaries, and unverified performance points are retained.

**upgrade39 has completed the implementation, review, and formal gatekeeping of this set of 30 scenarios. The full compatibility and delivery of Linux/Windows 0.3.0 is still pending.** The Windows/Linux real fixed workerd has passed 30/30 each, and all seven product running combinations have also passed. Currently, it is still in Debug build mode, with the Root HEAD remaining at `8028b88adff26cbc1abcb9a2fafce2ffc3d8090f`, package version 0.2.2, patch 58, with 4474 inputs; no submissions, pushes, or releases have been made, nor have the new three freeze ALL and unfiltered full matrix tasks been completed.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

Two actual discrepancies have been corrected. The regular inbound 101 now retains the initial Promise, timers, and I/O sources created by HTTP until the last accepted application connection ends. Previously, the same Promise would become inactive when created in the HTTP processor, but would remain functional when created within WebSocket events. New positive and negative examples confirm that the continuation continues to execute while the connection is active. After the last connection is closed, even if other HTTP requests call the saved resolver, the expired continuation cannot be restored. The original termination rules for regular HTTP and pure native proxies are retained.

The cancellation behavior of the native fetch response reader is now handled based on fixed workerd measurements: the reasons for rejected serialization of pending reads are processed, closed and cancel are completed, and then the read returns done, releasing the lock after releasingLock. Objects, default values, strings, Errors, and null are checked for their actual types and values; undefined should not be mistaken for the same "error without a name/message". The native reader accepts only one pending read, with the second returning a clear TypeError. The native callback sequence is closed → cancel → read-error; the standard JS flow is still read → closed → cancel. The quota wrapper retains the native flow source marker, and the Promise state bridge is read-only and does not execute user getters.

The fixed items 30 also cover the actual TLS IPv6/IP SAN and SNI, 180000 byte-native proxies, HTTP↔HTTPS and HTTPS→HTTPS redirection, target permissions and certificate errors. When holding the response body, cancel/abort, the response headers are pending, the original ClientHello is pending, the unfinished redirect body, and the final connection ends. The contract independently parses the original ClientHello's SNI, checks each byte of HTTP, masks, data and Close, the nonce at each hop, cross-source Authorization handling, and the actual TCP/HTTP quantity. Checks before cancellation and connection termination before service stop. There is no relaxation of certificate verification, and the secure callback is not considered as an HTTP request.

Both ends, 16 nodes, each verification module is **6173/6173**. There are no failures, cancellations, skips, or to-dos. New contract modules **968** have been added. ## Round 84 (2026-10-20): TLS of fetch, IPv6, redirection, and response body boundaries

The current **upgrade38** has completed the implementation of this set of boundaries and formal regression tests. **22 additional** fixed scenarios have been added, and the actual fixed versions of workerd on Windows/Linux are both **22/22**. The current seven product modes are also **22/22**. **The full Linux/Windows 0.3.0 compatibility and release are still not completed.** The Root HEAD remains at `8028b88adff26cbc1abcb9a2fafce2ffc3d8090f`, with a package version of **0.2.2** and a patch stack of **58**. Currently, **4466** inputs are being processed; no new three-fixed ALL scenarios or full matrix filtering has been executed.

| Current build | Directioned CTest | Complete required gate | Total Registrations / Required Registrations | New Boundary Product Pattern |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Windows Ordinary | 21/21 | 57/57 | 534 / 130 | single-worker, static-pool: 22/22 each |
| Windows ASan | 21/21 | 57/57 | 535 / 130 | single-worker, static-pool: 22/22 each |
| Linux UID 1000 | 22/22 | 72/72 | 759 / 150 | single-worker, static-pool, managed - 22/22 each |

The Host/App now shares the fetch authority parsing, truly supporting `[::1]:port`. The permission intersection is calculated after normalizing equivalent IPv6 spellings. Limited ports cannot be expanded to arbitrary ports, and adjacent addresses cannot inherit authorization. Abnormal parentheses, ports, address ranges, zones, and NUL embeddings are rejected, and no changes are made to the target object in case of failure. Windows is tested through real App/shared parser and policy compilation; Host hosting configuration tests are only performed on actually registered Linux builds.

The TLS digital address of fetch now sends SNIs that are consistent with the fixed workerd, while retaining the certificate chain and the host's valid certificate. In the IP scenario, the real SNI selects a trusted certificate with an IP SAN. Old products fail due to the omission of selecting the wrong certificate by the SNI. The test also covers trusted TLS upgrades, 180000 bytes of native proxies, duplicate Set-Cookie headers, incorrect hostnames, and untrusted certificates. The latter two require a real TLS connection to be established, and no HTTP requests should be sent. The number of TLS secure callbacks does not impersonate the number of HTTP requests, nor is it required that two TLS implementations have the same alarm callback timing.

`Request.redirect` only accepts `follow` / `manual`. Values that are not supported will cause a synchronous error during Request construction. The fetch method will return a rejected Promise, and no network access will be performed. The string hint conversion and the object identity thrown by the user are retained. Loop redirection precisely accesses 21 times. Errors include the complete accessed URL. Cross-host or cross-port redirection only removes Authorization, Proxy-Authorization, Cookie, and probe headers, which are retained according to a fixed reference. If the redirection response body is not fully read, it should wait. This is verified by a real reference, and the error is not converted into a cancellation of the response body.

In both cases of delayed delivery and simultaneous input of data, the response body first includes a valid 5 byte prefix. After that, it reports any associated internal errors related to truncated `Network connection lost.` or invalid chunks. Tags with empty 200, 409 with EOF-based boundaries, and 204/205/304’s bodyNull/bodyUsed all have explicit definitions. This round does not claim to fix the issue of “lost prefixes”: the old version still retains these 5 bytes. What has been corrected are the errors in classification and the textual descriptions provided.

The review also identified issues related to the initial draft: the shared initialization function applied the enumeration check of Request to Response. Ultimately, only the redirect was checked in the Request/fetch path, and Response/Response.json only read its own dictionary members. The formal parameter scenario included **13 Request constructions, 3 fetches, and 10 Response/Response.json observations**, confirming that irrelevant redirect values were neither converted nor triggered by getters.

The **15 Node verification modules each have 5169/5169** instances, with no failures, cancellations, skips, or to-dos. Among them, there are **640 new boundary modules**, **559 platform matrix modules**, and **202 archived gate modules**. In CTest, the `workerd_ws_gate_controls` combination involves multiple modules, totaling **456 instances**, which are not mixed with the 202 individual modules. The numbers have been verified from the complete original logs, and the truncated sections of JUnit stdout are not used for counting. The comparator checks the actual original requests and responses, complete data with Close, masks, each new nonce, duplicate headers, error numbers with stderr, certificate signatures and SAN, identical VMs, and the source end before service termination. The number of TCP connections to the target must be zero. Necessary platform matrices and complete gate modules have been integrated. The original Upgrade **13**, source lifespan **19**, and native proxy **22** instances remain.

Reverse verification using the original executable file: The previous round of results tested 22 new items, with only **10 items meeting the criteria**. The failure to start on IPv6 made the entire set of collected data incomplete; **it cannot be described as a complete execution pass or 12 independent defects**. The preliminary product collection included **22 items, 21 of which met the criteria**, but was rejected due to new parameter scenarios, accurately exposing Response issues that were not covered in previous tests. Failure logs, original inputs, old binaries, and pre-reference data were all retained. The first version of the comparator also missed calculating the body-first messages for the same batch of inputs, as well as two verification failures where the CTest path was incorrectly configured during Windows manual startup. This did not result in a reduction of product assertions to pass.

The evidence directory remains `build-v030-dev/evidence/native-asan-qualification-20261018/`. The final entry is `upgrade38-final-audit.json`; the actual build is `upgrade38v-build-*`, the complete gate is `upgrade38x-gate-*`, seven sets of dedicated runs are `upgrade38y-*`, dual-side verification is `upgrade38w-units-*`, the real reference is `upgrade38t-reference-*`, and reverse inspection of old products is `upgrade38u-negative-*`. **64 selected** binary and build metadata are retained, as well as **195** inputs related to builds/tests/runs; among them, 8 archived result documents correspond to the list before the update, and the subsequent 8 result documents are recorded separately. ## Round 83 (2026-10-20): Fetched 101 Responses. Native proxy and RequestInfo conversion.

The current candidate **upgrade37** has completed the native inbound/outbound coupling for common `return await fetch(upgradeRequest)` and repackaging of Response. There are **22** new formal proxy contracts, with **22/22** results for both fixed workerd and seven groups of current products. **Overall Linux/Windows 0.3.0 strong compatibility and release are still pending.** The Root HEAD remains at `8028b88adff26cbc1abcb9a2fafce2ffc3d8090f`, with a package version of **0.2.2** and a patch stack of **58**. Currently, there are **4458** inputs, and no new three-frozen ALLs or unfiltered full matrices have been executed.

The implementation verifies the same original I/O source, activates the inbound endpoint, and handles the fetched 101 endpoints that have not been accepted through a private native bridge. It then takes over the bidirectional messages and orderly closes them. The forwarding process retains the immutable `ChannelMessage` original lease and authority, reserves the sending quota, and copies the messages piece by piece. No complete JS intermediate buffer is created. When the source event and the peer's pending message exist, credit is temporarily replenished, allowing the assembly to continue even when the first message that exceeds the receiving window is larger. In case of an abnormal disconnection, the existing cancel/ACK mechanisms are used to clean up both ends. The outbound owner does not send any retain control that is only applicable to inbound sources. No additional changes have been made to the existing C ABI, v4 format, capability bits, or the 10-second drain deadline.

The native network pump does not maintain the original HTTP application context. The original request has been redirected to the existing source mechanism: There is no real truth at all accept Homologous genes JS During the sub-connection process, Timer, Not completed yet HTTP body, Hibernate Promise The continued version has been recovered; When such sub-connections exist, the original timer and sub-connection callbacks continue to operate. Eventually, after the JS owner closes the connection, any remaining tasks will be canceled. Transferred to the endpoint from a legitimate source `send()` / `close()` Ignored for sending `accept()` The report has been forwarded; All four APIs involved in the cross-request call reported source errors. The lifespan of systems that use ordinary, accepted sub-connections to replace native proxies is zero.

In the actual held-body scenario, it was also identified that there is a gap where legitimate `URL` inputs are rejected by the standard `fetch()` method. Currently, the RequestInfo is transformed once before the RequestInit getter, preserving the body/brand of the native Request and clone. The objects thrown by the custom string conversion are rejected asynchronously by fetch, while their identities are retained. The formal scenario covers URL, Request packaging, clone, transformation order, string hints, and exception identities. The fixture has not been changed to a string to circumvent the issue.

## Round 82 (2026-10-20): Managed Local Source Retention and Windows Cache Migration

The current candidate **upgrade36** has closed the specific gap left by the managed updates from the previous round, and has fixed the MSVC zero-dependency object cache issues identified during review. **The overall Linux/Windows 0.3.0 strong compatibility and release are still incomplete.** The Root HEAD remains at `8028b88adff26cbc1abcb9a2fafce2ffc3d8090f`, with the package version at **0.2.2** and the patch stack at **58**. The current main project has **4449** inputs; there are no new three-fractional freeze ALL or unfiltered full matrix entries.

After the remote EOF, the Worker has correctly retained the native timers in the local open direction. However, the Host only monitors the physical channels and HTTP inflight status. After the channel cancellation ACK is completed, these counts become zero. Updating to version 2 will immediately terminate the old processes that still hold the v1 timers. It is necessary to first confirm that all transmission/dialing/HTTP metrics are zero before performing the update, to avoid relying on additional pauses in HTTP to temporarily fix the old processes.

The implementation adds an optional `CAP_CONNECTION_LIFETIME` **128**, which depends on UPGRADE and CONFIRM (including DUPLEX/A2), and does not depend on FETCH_UPGRADE; the known capability mask is **255**. The first-party request/actual value is **251**, and HALF_CLOSE is still not declared. The READY results for the old requests 0/31/63 remain 0/27/59; the true negotiation for the new values 123/155/251/255 has also been tested. The new frame `kConnectionLifetime = 28` is valid only in v4, Worker→Host, and with zero flags/request id, with the payload fixed as `u32 channel_id + u32 retained(0|1)`.

After a Worker receives a DISCONNECT message from an established inbound connection, if the destination is still available, it must first send a RETAIN request before canceling the ACK response. Once the destination is closed and any pending tasks are cleared, they are released. The Registry separates collection entities from physical channels, ensuring strict verification of capabilities, sources, timing, duplicates, unknown IDs, payloads, and a maximum of **2×max_channels**. Physical ACKs continue to release network resources, while shutdowns clear any retained collections. Event C **19** is added to the enumeration, preserving all old structural layouts. The `credit` value reflects the total number of retained resources after range checks. Hosts use this mechanism to manage physical channels and retained sources, avoiding mixed snapshots caused by ACK interleaving. Both conditions for early exit in GenerationPool are utilized, with the original drain deadline remaining unchanged. For more details, please refer to the current documentation. [FetchRPC Contract](../../reference/fetchrpc-v4.md#32b-optional-connection-lifetime-2026-10-20).

In this round, the inbound transmission will be disconnected, the local sending direction will be closed, and the context resources will be reclaimed separately. After the remote EOF, the coupled inbound endpoints that have not closed their local sending direction will continue to maintain their original state. The original handler can complete the waiting for HTTP responses or native timers. After the local close, the current synchronization code and queued microtasks will be cleared, and the last remaining timers and unfinished resources will be canceled. The constructor/fetched endpoints will follow the behavior confirmed in the previous round. There are no modifications to the C ABI, protocol layout, capability bits, or patch stack in this round.

## Round 80 (2026-10-20): Wait events when the parent connection is disconnected and local Close status

The current candidate **upgrade34** fixes two practical differences: when the parent connection is abnormally disconnected, it cannot terminate the waiting event that is still maintained by the accepted child connection from the same origin; calling `close()` in an abnormal Close callback should still end the local sending direction and update the readyState. The formal lifecycle contract is expanded from 12 to **17**, and both ends actually lock the workerd and the current product mode is **17/17**. The three complete required gates have actually been passed by **49/49, 49/49, 62/62**, with no failures or skips. **Overall, 0.3.0 is strongly compatible and the release is still incomplete; another confirmed gap where close() is called late is mentioned below.**Root HEAD / Package version remains at `8028b88adff26cbc1abcb9a2fafce2ffc3d8090f` / **0.2.2**, and the previous complete candidate is still **compat27b**.

Before the fix, the broken branch marked the still-awaiting parent event as terminal. This would trigger a **`terminal continuation leak`** and terminate the worker, even when its native fetch is still held by the same-origin child socket. The `src/worker_runtime.cc` file now checks whether there are still elevated connections available at the point where the termination branch is executed. Existing HTTP activity checks, cancellation barriers, and resource recycling budgets are retained. In scenarios where there is no surviving source and the application has ended the local sending direction, resources will still be recycled. It is not possible to completely prohibit all abnormal recycling.

`js/websocket.js` After disconnection from the inbound pairing endpoint `close()` Continue to examine the context and record that the local sending direction has been closed. The constructor and fetched endpoints maintain the outbound behavior referenced, and the close() function after disconnection does not change the transmission direction additionally. The actual reference can be observed within the exception Close callback **OPEN(1) → CLOSING(2)**. This is the local API status, not sending illegal Close frames over a disconnected TCP connection. The normal Close callback is still set to CLOSED 3). There are no changes to the C ABI, protocol layout, or capability bits this round.

The five new formal scenarios include: completing pending events when the parent connection remains open, continuing to wait after the parent connection is normally closed, continuing to wait when the parent connection is abnormally disconnected but the child connection remains alive, and recycling pending requests when there are no child connections and `close()` is called in the abnormal Close callback. The first three scenarios verify the real 409 response entity, handler continuation, timer, and the bidirectional Close/EOF of the final child connection. The last scenario verifies that the pending request actually reaches EOF, the continuation remains unfinished, and the timer is paused. A new scenario where the fetched endpoint is actively disconnected is added, which independently verifies its readyState in callbacks, microtasks, and timers, and clearly records the action of the upstream fixture actively terminating the transmission. The original request/nonce, masked frames, state in the same realm, non-modifiable snapshots, endpoint state, and normal process exit are retained. The recording of actively disconnecting the parent client is clearly labeled as `client-disposed`, and local actions are not disguised as remote EOF.

## Round 79 (2026-10-20): Upgrade fetch shares I/O lifecycle and complete mandatory gate checks

Current Working Candidates **upgrade33** The resource recycling for the new Upgrade fetch has been corrected, and a complete required gate has been successfully completed in Windows standard, Windows ASan, and Linux standard user configurations: **49/49, 49/49, 62/62** None have failed or been skipped. The new lifecycle contract actually locks the workerd at both ends and in seven product patterns respectively **12/12**. **0.3.0 Overall strong compatibility and release are still incomplete.** There are currently no new frozen source code versions for ALL builds, nor is there any filtering of the full matrix. The previous complete candidate is still **compat27b**. Root HEAD / Package Version remains `8028b88adff26cbc1abcb9a2fafce2ffc3d8090f` / **0.2.2**.

This round corrected an assumption from the previous round's plan: the resource lifespan follows the entire I/O context originating from the same request. A successfully accepted sub-WebSocket can maintain suspended fetches, parent event continuations, and timers under that origin; these resources cannot be immediately canceled when the original parent connection is closed. Only after the last elevated connection has ended is the unfinished handshakes, HTTP bodies, and unaccepted endpoints reclaimed from that origin. The independence between the two owners remains. The previous approach of "exactly binding to create connections and canceling them when the connections are closed" was only successful in **9/10** of the extended scenarios, and was canceled in advance on `same-origin-parent-pending`. This failure, along with its product binaries and inputs, are retained. It has been replaced by the actual two-way reference rules, and the old plan cannot be continued.

Implementation is centralized in `src/worker_runtime.cc`: it tracks the status of connected devices and resources that have been upgraded. It also determines whether the connection still belongs to another accepted device. Timers, native HTTP clients, and new fetch dials are managed through shared sources for resource recycling. Existing results, cancellation barriers, queue budgets, and late callback mechanisms are retained. Even if the parent transport owner is deleted, its processes can continue to run within the same origin-connected devices. HTTP requests that have not yet been completed will continue to follow the existing HTTP lifecycle. The C ABI and protocol layout remain unchanged, and the first-party capabilities specified in 64 are still enabled.

Newly established formal contracts 12 Classes are…: The handshake process will be terminated upon completion of the source process, I have read the text body, Unread messages body, Not yet accept socket, Already done accept socket Survive independently, double owner Isolation, reader cancel, body abort, Same-origin suspension created by the parent event fetch, Same-origin suspension created by sub-event fetch, And the actual response completion after the parent connections are closed under these two sources, along with the continuation of the timer operations. Original request, Each time it’s independent nonce, Repetitive headers, Status, Complete frames, Close ACK, Realistic EOF, The same realm Status, Both the timer increment and the final stop point are checked independently; Existing requests or frames cannot be modified between snapshots. Using equal lengths at both ends as the only criterion is not applicable.

The gatekeepers continued to explicitly retain existing deviations, including constructors `unrequested-subprotocol`, invalid Close code, and bounded sending queues, which are existing contract exceptions. The gatekeepers cannot be written as having no difference in standard semantics.

In actual execution, **13 Node verifiers/gate modules were used, with 3653/3653 each**, resulting in 0 failures, cancellations, skips, and to-dos. The following will continue in the order below. The 12 scenarios of this round should not be considered as part of a complete closed-loop lifecycle:

1. **Continuing to address real-life lifecycle issues.** This includes situations where the original parent connection is disconnected unexpectedly, while the asynchronous handler is still waiting for responses. It also involves contexts where HTTP requests have been completed, but the child sockets remain active. There are also issues related to cross-context accept/resource transfers, timeouts, delayed ACCEPT/EOF signals, conflicts between credit/ACK/abort requests, large-scale resource starvation situations, as well as reentrancy issues during recycling processes, and scenarios where budgets run out. The `queue_connection_close()` function provides a dedicated mechanism for handling events that have not yet been settled upon closure. It is necessary to perform real-time processing on both sides of the connection before deciding whether any adjustments are needed. The current acceptance candidate has been approved **http25a**, three sets **4,424 Single-byte project inputs** The strict ALL build has passed. In the previous round of supplementary processing, all three sets of unfiltered data were frozen. Specifically, there were **1,749 items**: 1,705 items passed, 21 items failed, and 23 items were skipped. On Windows operating systems, 509 items met the requirements (493/6/10); on ASan platforms, 510 items met the requirements (491/9/10); on Linux systems, 730 items met the requirements (721/6/3). The complete record can be found in the file `io24d-current-full-matrix.json`. This record cannot be replaced by the historical data of 1,672 items, nor can the current task be considered as having passed all three sets of data.

The cooperative deadline, Hono/h3/Elysia's lifecycle, failed in all three sets. The lifecycle of itty-router's autorouter/router/manual also failed additionally on ASan. The native count shows that after the timeout, there is still an asynchronous resource for the old request, with no pending tasks remaining. Normally, explicitly canceling the request context in which it is located would be performed, but the abort bridge for the deadline does not have this capability. `clearTimeout()` does not clear the timer for this request due to the lack of the current owner. Subsequently, resource recycling will consider the Worker as still having tasks that require response.

The correction involves placing the abort bridge for the deadline within the `expired_token` context, and restoring the original context after returning, consistent with an explicit cancel operation. The rules for maintaining deadlines, determining ownership, handling errors, and concluding tasks are preserved. First, the prototype is verified independently using Linux for compilation and linking. If four failures occur, the original continuation/request context is protected. Then, the current source code is merged, and all seven scenarios are verified using both standard and ASan, as well as the project's TSan. No results were obtained by changing the expected value, extending the time limit, or discarding the first failure.

The candidate has been approved for acceptance. **io24d**: Windows Release, Windows ASan, Linux Release/LTO Use it **4, 424 File containing items and bytes** Three sets of strict ALL configurations have been approved. Linux/Windows 0.3.0 The overall compatibility and approval process for the release are still incomplete.**

Before the fix, after calling `fetch()` in the WebSocket A message callback, `A.send()` was executed immediately. Both products reported an error stating "different requests cannot perform I/O". The workerd was locked on both ends, and the messages were sent normally. The native HTTP connection check only reads the Binding resource owner; since the User runtime does not have this owner, it skips the check when entering the context, but clears the current request when exiting.

`0055-http-policy-caller-context.patch` retains the resource owner priority that remains unchanged during Binding; when there is no Binding owner, it uses the asynchronous context captured by the client. In the absence of both, it checks to follow the current caller. The entry and restoration of context are kept paired. This correction also covers both synchronous URI checks and the actual address checks after DNS delivery, without changing the connection permission determination.

DNS corrections also follow [RFC 6761 §6.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6761.html#section-6.4): The Host parsing library immediately gives an asynchronous negative result for `invalid.` and its subdomains, without sending any external DNS packets. Names are matched according to ASCII case and optional root points, using full tag boundaries; no branches are written for a particular test domain name. The original error assertions and dialing budgets are retained. New native mandatory controls are added for coverage of case sensitivity, subdomains, root points, cancellation/destruction of queued completion, absence of actual DNS packets, real A/AAAA for similar common domain names, real NXDOMAIN, and complete dialer cause codes/resource balancing; the original DNS phase-by-phase cancellation and timeout controls remain mandatory. `io24c-failure-audit.json` retains failed scenarios and 21 actual inputs. `io24d-dns-regression.json` records positive controls for Linux original products and separate compiled/linked variant behaviors: only the retained domain return path is removed, resulting in a causal assertion rejection. The production source code and product bytes remain unchanged. Repeats are not used as a substitute for fixes.

The number of formal shared resource scenarios has increased from 29 to **31**. The old 29 scenarios will continue to be retained. Two new actual request paths, hostname/numeric, have been added. Both A and B real WebSocket connections are established. Connection B remains alive but does not initiate fetch requests. Connection A attempts to send data to both connections during the call, after returning, and in the asynchronous response callback. The sending to one connection must be successful, while the other connection must receive a complete standard error. The assertion checks all original frames, the independent nonce of two Upgrades, Close ACK, actual EOF, the state of the same realm, and the checkpoints before and after B's termination.

Three new CTests have been added to native testing: `tjs_fetch_policy_async_context`, `tjs_fetch_policy_binding_context`, and `tjs_fetch_policy_unowned_context`. The independent hooks ensure that the Binding owner A is different from the callback context B. The connection check prioritizes A, while the callback still uses B. Additionally, when there is no Binding owner, B is used, and the client with no owner retains the current caller. Each item includes negative controls for explicit rejection, positive controls for sending actual HTTP requests after DNS, ownership before and after the call, synchronous abort callbacks, and balanced TCP closure with capture/release. Another caller of the Binding still rejects reading client properties. The original DNS cancellation and pending handshake tests also include new synchronous open ownership assertions.

<a id="capsid-review-phase-72"></a>
## Current progress of round 72 (2026-10-19): fetch resource recycling and cancellation semantics

The candidate **resource23g** has been approved. Three sets of frozen source code contain **4,423 byte-per-item files**, and all strict ALL builds on Windows Release, Windows ASan, and Linux Release/LTO have passed. This round of updates compared to **cross22g** include **25 implementation changes, build locks, and test inputs**. Additionally, eight results documents from the 71st round have been incorporated into the frozen inputs; the patch stack has increased to **54**. The Root HEAD remains at `8028b88adff26cbc1abcb9a2fafce2ffc3d8090f`, and the package version is still **0.2.2**. This round's resource-specific work has been approved, but the overall compatibility with Linux/Windows 0.3.0 and the release approval are still pending.

Implementation and Coverage:

- `0051-connection-http-resource-lifetime.patch` maintains a registry without additional JS roots for unfinished native HTTP clients. When the connection to the resource ends, only the matching creation context is canceled; DNS and socket cleanup continue to release their references, and the cancellation process does not execute application callbacks. The global collection of silent cancel clients that are held by the fetch module will be removed. The original rule of "outbound connection ended, original HTTP still waiting for callback" is retained.
- `0052-workerd-fetch-abort-exceptions.patch` distinguishes between cancellations that are canceled before invocation and those that are in progress. The former retains the original reason; the latter uses the exception snapshot saved at the time of abort, with each consumer receiving a independently restored value. The existing exception value conversion is reused to cover default reasons, strings, Error, DOMException, null, numbers, ordinary objects, post-modification, and two consumers with the same signal. [Save state and native cancellation paths in the workerd source code](https://github.com/cloudflare/workerd/blob/8f311ae3e3cf3064983f56a6a1c8b7ee33d6b99f/src/workerd/api/basics.c%2B%2B#L900) Also used for verification.
- `0053-synchronous-http-abort-checkpoint.patch` fixes the issue of premature emptying of microtasks within synchronous native abort. This reentrance still enters the original resource context and holds references independently. Microtasks and connection cleanup checkpoints wait for the outer JS call to return. The lost tail of the response body and close events have been constrained by the assertion of the complete frame, synchronous code sequence, and microtask order.
- `0054-disabled-cookie-cache-path.patch` handles empty Cookie paths by disabling persistent caching. System call tracing revealed that the system previously treated empty strings as file names, causing repeated creation of `.LCK` files by the client every time it started a new session, which would block the managed restricted directory for about five seconds. After the fix, this file is no longer cached. A new guard is added to the `.LCK` real directory to verify the file identity before use. The original three-second upstream waiting limit is still applied to detect old artifacts, without increasing the budget.
- Multiple fetch port configurations on the same host are first merged, sorted, and deduplicated. Then, the host range is verified to generate a stable rule ID. The separated and merged configurations produce the same results as the summary; invalid ports are still rejected, and a limited port range cannot cover any-port requests.
- Added **29** official shared resource scenarios: response header waiting/response body reading at connection closure, active reader.cancel, seven reasons for each of the three cancellation phases, dual connection isolation, reason snapshot, and dual consumers. The upstream server records the complete original request, actual EOF, and event sequence; the upstream socket is not destroyed before completion. WebSocket checks for actual Upgrade, all original frames, Close ACK, actual transport termination, the same realm, state after closure, and cooperative exit.
